Given a code patch (git diff) and a test name, \\\"\\n    \\\"predict whether the test will pass or fail when run against the patched code.\\\"\\n)\\nUSER_TEMPLATE = \\\"## Patch (git diff)\\\\n{code}\\\\n\\\\n## Test\\\\n{test}\\\\n\\\\nWill this test pass or fail?\\\"\\nLABEL_MAP = {1: \\\"pass\\\", 0: \\\"fail\\\"}\\n\\n\\ndef load_jsonl(path):\\n    with open(path) as f:\\n        return [json.loads(line) for line in f]\\n\\n\\ndef detect_thinking_support(tokenizer):\\n    try:\\n        tokenizer.apply_chat_template(\\n            [{\\\"role\\\": \\\"user\\\", \\\"content\\\": \\\"hi\\\"}],\\n            tokenize=False, enable_thinking=False,\\n        )\\n        return {\\\"enable_thinking\\\": False}\\n    except TypeError:\\n        return {}\\n\\n\\ndef build_prompt_ids(tokenizer, code, test, tmpl_kw, max_len):\\n    user_msg = USER_TEMPLATE.format(code=code, test=test)\\n    msgs = [\\n        {\\\"role\\\": \\\"system\\\", \\\"content\\\": SYSTEM_PROMPT},\\n        {\\\"role\\\": \\\"user\\\", \\\"content\\\": user_msg},\\n    ]\\n    ids = tokenizer.apply_chat_template(\\n        msgs, tokenize=True, add_generation_prompt=True, **tmpl_kw,\\n    )\\n    if hasattr(ids, \\\"input_ids\\\"):\\n        ids = ids.input_ids\\n    if not isinstance(ids, list):\\n        ids = list(ids)\\n    truncated = len(ids) > max_len\\n    if truncated:\\n        ids = ids[:max_len]\\n    return ids, truncated\\n\\n\\ndef parse_prediction(text):\\n    if \\\"&lt;/think>\\\" in text:\\n        text = text.split(\\\"&lt;/think>\\\")[-1]\\n    text = text.strip().lower()\\n    if text.startswith(\\\"pass\\\"):\\n        return 1\\n    if text.startswith(\\\"fail\\\"):\\n        return 0\\n    if \\\"pass\\\" in text and \\\"fail\\\" not in text:\\n        return 1\\n    if \\\"fail\\\" in text and \\\"pass\\\" not in text:\\n        return 0\\n    return -1\\n\\n\\ndef load_model(checkpoint_dir, base_model_name, device):\\n    from peft import PeftModel\\n    from transformers import AutoModelForCausalLM, AutoTokenizer\\n\\n    tokenizer = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(\\n        base_model_name, trust_remote_code=True, padding_side=\\\"left\\\",\\n    )\\n    if tokenizer.pad_token is None:\\n        tokenizer.pad_token = tokenizer.eos_token\\n\\n    try:\\n        import flash_attn\\n        attn_impl = \\\"flash_attention_2\\\"\\n    except ImportError:\\n        attn_impl = \\\"sdpa\\\"\\n    log.info(f\\\"Attention: {attn_impl}\\\")\\n\\n    base_model = AutoModelForCausalLM.from_pretrained(\\n        base_model_name,\\n        torch_dtype=torch.bfloat16,\\n        trust_remote_code=True,\\n        attn_implementation=attn_impl,\\n    )\\n    model = PeftModel.from_pretrained(base_model, checkpoint_dir)\\n    model.eval()\\n    model = model.to(device)\\n    return model, tokenizer\\n\\n\\ndef run_inference(model, tokenizer, test_data, batch_size, max_seq_length, device):\\n    tmpl_kw = detect_thinking_support(tokenizer)\\n    total = len(test_data)\\n    label_counts = Counter(ex[\\\"label\\\"] for ex in test_data)\\n    majority_class = 1 if label_counts.get(1, 0) >= label_counts.get(0, 0) else 0\\n\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, <details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I have all the information. Let me note something important:

The CWM script has D077 auto-set logic:
- If `max_seq_length` is None and split is "val", it auto-sets to 8192
- If `max_seq_length` is None and split is "test", it auto-sets to 2048

But we want val@5120, not val@8192. So we must explicitly pass `--max_seq_length 5120`.

Also for CLS, the batch_size logic isn't auto-set — it defaults to 16. 

For CWM, since we're passing max_seq_length=5120 which exceeds 2048, batch_size will auto-set to 2 if we don't specify it, which works fine. But for CLS with that same sequence length, I should reduce batch_size from 16 to 8 to be safe with memory constraints, especially since CLS only generates 8 tokens. I need to check how CLS handles the output path — it joins the script directory with the args.output argument, so I need to make sure the directory exists beforehand since CLS won't create it automatically, unlike CWM which has its own output directory handling. I'm realizing the relative paths in the CLS case are actually relative to the script directory, so I should use absolute paths for CWM output to avoid confusion. Now I'm organizing the evaluation sweep, starting with the CLS v5-conda checkpoint at 5120 sequence length with various batch sizes and device configurations. Now I'm setting up the evaluation commands for CWM seed123 across multiple checkpoints at the same sequence length. I need to clarify which checkpoints to actually run for both CWM variants — v12 has checkpoints 300-700 available while seed123 has 200-600, so I should confirm the sweep strategy rather than running all of them. the batch_size will automatically be set to 2 since 5120 exceeds the 2048 threshold. Now I need to write out the commands file to the server at `/home/sonny/exec-sim-repair-verifier/val5120_sweep_commands.txt`, and I'm thinking through how to parallelize across the two available GPUs once the CWM evaluation finishes.

Looking at the workload: the CLS v5 checkpoints are quick runs with batch_size=8 and minimal token generation, while the CWM variants are much heavier with batch_size=2 and 1024 tokens each. With 13 total runs across 2 GPUs, I can execute them in parallel pairs—pairing a fast CLS run with a slow CWM run on separate GPUs to balance the load, then moving through the remaining checkpoints sequentially. I'm realizing CLS runs are actually fast enough to batch sequentially on one GPU while letting CWM handle the other, so I should organize the sweep to run all CLS checkpoints together and parallelize the slower CWM jobs across both devices. I'll write out a clear script with individual commands and comments to make this execution plan concrete.

Now I need to handle CLS output directory creation since it doesn't auto-makedirs, and I'm also checking whether the base model path in the adapter config will be used correctly — the load_model function pulls from args.model_name rather than reading it from the adapter config, so I should verify that Qwen/Qwen3-4B is accessible or if I need to use the cached path instead.
```
